## Document 261: AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

AI boom or doom: what will 
superintelligence feel like and  
when will we get there?

1 
Authors 
Adrian Cox 
Research Analyst

Azeem Azhar, Exponential View 
Expert Collaborator

October 12, 2026 
The recent panic about rogue AI agents collaborating in swarms to take over the 
internet and threaten human existence has, more than occasionally, lacked 
nuance. But it does reflect how artificial intelligence is progressing in creative 
and surprising ways, and at a pace that seems uncontrollable. 
For some in the industry, it is evidence that the technology is following a path of 
accelerating self-improvement that will lead to artificial general intelligence (AGI) 
and, perhaps, to utopia or catastrophe. 
Former Google CEO Eric Schmidt has sketched out a “San Francisco 
Consensus” based on a belief in scaling laws, with ever larger AI models 
becoming an input into their own improvement; superintelligence by the end of 
the decade; and transformative AI systems outperforming humans on many 
tasks. This loop would lead to exponential growth in scientific advancement, 
financial returns and human progress. And crucially, AGI, and then 
superintelligence, would merely be milestones in a process, far from its climax. 
If that were the case, superintelligence could make much of the cognitive work 
that dominates the world economy much cheaper and change the ways in which 
firms compete. But its economic effects will depend on how businesses and 
customers respond. 
In this paper, we analyse what the San Francisco Consensus means for investors 
and enterprises in the years to come and what could accelerate – or slow down – 
its timeline of AGI by 2030. 
1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
3. Can we get there practically? 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
The question is urgent. 
And it is complicated by reports of AI models autonomously accessing the 
internet and hacking other companies; a public backlash over the energy-
intensive data centres springing up as part of a historic $1 trillion a year 
investment boom; and dire warnings of threats to life if superintelligent AI runs 
roughshod over its human creators to achieve its own goals.

### Page 2

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
1.1 What are AGI and superintelligence? 
Artificial general intelligence (AGI) is a simple milestone scribbled in pencil: an AI 
system that has surpassed human capabilities. Superintelligence is the 
continuum beyond that point. It is not merely systems that exceed the best 
human performance across one cognitive task or another, but system s that 
exceed human potential across every cognitive task. 
The San Francisco Consensus takes this further: the intelligence must be able to 
produce more intelligence. The key to getting there is so -called recursive self -
improvement, where the AI is able to autonomously design and improve itself in 
a way that compounds exponentially. A first step has maybe already been taken, 
with OpenAI saying recently that AI is now writing approximately 80 percent of 
the company’s code. 
There is significant disagreement on how we will be able to identify AGI or 
superintelligence when we see it, let alone what comes afterwards.  
OpenAI’s Charter defines AGI in economic terms, as “highly autonomous systems 
that outperform humans at the most economically valuable work”. Google 
researchers proposed a five -step ladder based on performance and generality, 
with level 5 “superhuman” for a system that outperforms 100% of humans. And 
philosopher Nick Bostrom, who got the ball rolling in 2014 with his influential 
book “Superintelligenc e”, defined it as “any intellect that greatly exceeds the 
cognitive performance of humans in virtually all domains of interest”. 
It’s also a moving target.  
In August, OpenAI CEO Sam Altman said the company would have an internal 
system that he would call AGI by later this year but subsequently told a podcast 
that “at best it’s a very poorly defined term. I was going to say it’s like an irrelevant 
marketing term”. Yet his No. 2 Greg Brockman described the new Astra model at 
the start of September as being potentially the moment that “AGI was created”. 
For this report, we have chosen to focus on a system that outperforms essentially 
all humans across essentially all cognitive tasks, including the development of AI 
itself.  
Under our scenario, by 2030 the best performing AI models would have the 
qualities of “superintelligence”, that is better than human experts in all domains. 
These would be frontier models: and using them would feel exactly like bringing 
the best human experts in a room to solve whatever business problem you faced.

And the equivalents of today’s frontier models would be available, quite literally, 
everywhere, compressed enough to run on every phone, tablet or laptop 
computer.  
In early 2026, we already see early examples of what highly capable AI can do. 
They give a flavour for a world with superintelligence even if the reality would be 
a leap beyond, both quantitatively and qualitatively. At that point, 
superintelligence would mark a regime break, not a faster version of what we 
already have. Rather than blindly doing what humans specify, but just a bit better, 
it would specify the task itself.

### Page 3

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

Figure 1: What changes when cognitive work gets abundantly cheaper?

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Meanwhile cost may be less and less of a constraint as ever greater machine 
intelligence becomes cheaply available. Of measured technologies, no 
technology has got cheaper faster than AI has, almost halving in price every 
quarter for the past three years on a like-for-like basis, or 13 times per year, Epoch 
data show. 
If these systems can be supplied widely, firms will be able to buy more of the 
complex, expensive and rare analysis, design and engineering kinds of services 
than they do today. Activities that were previously uneconomic could become 
viable, and in a competitive market, savings could pass to customers. The main 
driver will then be whether demand grows fast enough to preserve margins. 
However, superior capability, doesn’t, on its own, automatically lead to the 
adoption of cheap, abundant intelligence. Tasks that do not yet yield to super-

### Page 4

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

intelligence, perhaps because they require real world-coordination, will therefore 
end up commanding significant premia. 
At that point, cognitive labour stops being scarce and the bottleneck becomes 
the physical and practical assets that cannot be conjured by up more compute, 
such as electricity, land, steel and permits. 
1.2 Capability and diffusion 
If AI becomes capable of improving itself, the technology may compound faster 
and faster. The supply of machine intelligence could grow faster than business 
and physical infrastructure can accommodate.  
This balance between capability and diffusion creates different scenarios.

Figure 2: The balance between capabilities and diffusion

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Lower capability, constrained diffusion: where we are now 
Progress is steady and sector-by-sector. This is how technology usually diffuses, 
according to researchers for the New York Fed back in 2003, who tracked 20 
major technologies across leading industrial economies from 1788 and found “a 
pattern of trickle-down diffusion that is remarkably robust across technologies”. 
Lower capability, high diffusion: the cloud transition 
This is faster because the complementary investment is small, with no retooling 
and no skills at scale required. Amazon Web Services launched the modern cloud 
in 2006, offering scalable computing and storage infrastructure. Adoption grew 
and then accelerated over the pandemic. Almost all enterprises now use it  in 
some form.

### Page 5

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Higher capability, lower diffusion: the overhang 
This is theoretically possible and arguably where we are today in narrow domains. 
The technology would be applied in some sectors of the economy, while other 
sectors would move at a slower speed.  Superintelligent or not, it risks being 
bogged down by physical constraints, bureaucracy and human inertia. 
Higher capability, higher diffusion: superintelligence 
In this scenario, capability becomes so high that it changes the dynamics of 
diffusion itself. AI generates not just better AI (so -called recursive self -
improvement, or RSI) but also speeds up the integration process. 
Rapid AI diffusion could work two ways. 
In the first, AI could be enlisted to do integration work itself: mapping processes, 
rebuilding data work, retraining staff, writing the control and audit layer, and so 
on. The problem with this is that absorbing and recreating undocumented 
knowledge is impossible by definition, at least for a task-based superintelligence.  
The second way that higher capability AI could be rapidly diffused is much more 
competitive – and disruptive. Rather than incumbents slowly converting AI, 
perhaps using it for some of the hard work of integration, they could be instead 
displaced by new firms that have been built AI-native from day one. New entrants 
will never have to incur integration costs because there is nothing to integrate. 
Incumbents will need to change – fast – or die.  
Change could also be forced by earlier adopters in the supply chain, as happened 
with one company after another adopting electronic data interchange (EDI) to 
link with its suppliers or customers, or by a migration of capital towards early 
adopters, reprici ng the cost of capital for slow -coaches and prodding them to 
move faster. 
1.3 The forcing mechanism: change or die 
A company that uses superintelligence may outperform one that doesn’t. 
Firms using superintelligence will likely have substantial cost and revenue -
harvesting advantages. Every firm will therefore have to adopt superintelligence 
or retrench to a smaller set of offerings. And any business line of lasting value 
abandoned by a no n-adopter will be taken over by an adopter. Customer inertia 
will provide a safe harbour for a limited time only.  
The analogy here is electricity. There came a point in the deployment of 
electricity where firms in every sector, from entertainment to finance, from 
healthcare to transport, had to adopt the technology to remain relevant.  
Under this scenario, wait -and-see will not be an option. There will be no stable 
“non-adoption” alternative. Sufficiently high capabilities will force a tipping point 
of rapid diffusion and competitive repricing. Firms using yesterday’s production 
model will either have to change, choose a different market or die.  
Of course, caveats apply.  
Customers may not change behaviour immediately. They may even prefer 
companies which limit their use of AI, in the same way that some customers 
choose to avoid factory-produced food.

### Page 6

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

The paradox that Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Solow observed in 1987, 
that “you can see the computer age everywhere but in the productivity statistics”, 
is likely to still apply for a while – even with superintelligence. 
The reason is that the productivity J -curve identified by Stanford University AI 
economy guru Erik Brynjolfsson et al is unavoidable. In that scenario, the drag of 
early intangible investments to integrate general purpose technologies means 
that productivity initially appears to slow before rapidly accelerating.

Figure 3: Productivity J-curve: slow then fast

Source: Brynjolfsson E, Rock D, Syverson C, “The Productivity J-Curve” (2018, revised 2020), Deutsche Bank Research

Furthermore, “change or die” would require abundant capital and low regulatory 
entry points for nimble new entrants. But in practice, highly regulated areas such 
as banking, utilities, pharmaceuticals and defence are tough nuts to crack. 
Existing contracts and licences, high switching costs and decades of depreciated 
capital investment and infrastructure would shield incumbents at least for a 
healthy transition period. 
For an extreme example from the real world of better, cheaper technology failing 
to force change, take Pakistan’s 3.6 -gigawatt imported coal power plant fleet 
installed in the mid -2010s. In that case, it came with expensive take -or-pay 
liabilities which wi ll continue to run for decades, even though changes like 
cheaper rooftop solar have cut utilisation to below 20 percent.  
So: superintelligence will not force adoption everywhere. It will hit hardest in 
contestable sectors, say, high -volume customer services, routine software 
engineering and freelance creative work, and widen the gap between them and 
protected ones. Forced adoption will be about relative value, not an apocalypse.

### Page 7

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

1.4 What survives and what it means for portfolios 
Because AI sits on an aggressive learning curve, its price on an equivalent quality 
basis is plummeting. In a world of recursive super intelligence this is likely to 
continue. Its price will fall to the cost of compute.  
What will retain its value will be whatever the model cannot replicate: 
1. Proprietary data 
2. The right to operate , particularly in heavily regulated sectors such as 
healthcare and finance. 
3. Sticky customer relationships,  such as those where mission -critical 
workflows and databases are deeply entwined. 
4. Excludable physical assets, such as machinery and land. 
5. In addition, humans have proven to have unlimited wants.  Each shift in 
technological capabilities or cultural norms has delivered new needs to 
be met. Some will be needs we can articulate today, such as originality or 
empathy, but new ones are likely to arise.  
If this forcing mechanism holds, firms will separate into two “bimodal” groups: 
adopters and casualties, with very little in between. 
This dispersion of performance may create challenges for investors. The four 
years since the launch of ChatGPT in November 2022 have seen the stocks of 
semiconductor and semiconductor equipment makers surge almost seven times 
while those of software makers  have only risen by 80 percent. Conventional  
diversification and volatility-based allocation may become ineffective strategies.

Figure 4: Total return for S&P 500 semiconductor vs software stocks since 
launch of ChatGPT on Nov 30, 2022

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 8

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Diversification normally works because idiosyncratic risks are unrelated and 
average each other out. Here they would not: with one variable that overrides 
everything else, spreading capital across more names would just spread exposure 
rather than diluting it. 
Investors will need to consider adapting their approach in three ways: 
1. Classify investments as adopters or casualties rather than position off 
averages.  
• Under a normal distribution, errors are symmetric and partially 
cancel each other out. But under bimodality they do not: to put a firm 
in one category or another means getting it completely right or 
wrong. Position size should track confidence in the classification. 
2. Avoid reading calm price action as low risk.  
• Trailing volatility measures dispersion that has already been realised. 
Bimodal separation is a jump, where the two modes look identical 
right until the moment when they don’t. A classic example is Chegg, 
an educational software maker which traded like a normal small-cap 
stock until one day in May 2023 when it halved on a single day after 
management linked slowing growth in new customers to ChatGPT. 
3. Averaging down, ie  scooping up shares whose price has fallen, may no 
longer be prudent. 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
2.1 Scaling laws 
Scaling laws have provided the blueprint for improving AI over the past decade. 
It was a relationship between the amount of data, the amount of compute and 
the complexity of an AI model somewhat predictably informed how powerful a 
model would become. Google DeepMind’s Chinchilla model in 2022 showed that 
you could compensate for having a smaller model by using substantially more 
data. 
Scaling is older than the chatbot. In 1950 Claude Shannon built Theseus, a 
mechanical mouse that learned its way through a maze by trial and error. Epoch 
AI reckons it took about 40 operations of compute, known as FLOPs, to train. Grok 
4, trained in 2025, took roughly ten trillion trillion times as much. Across those 25 
orders of magnitude, more compute has brought more capability.

• A falling price may be information that a business model is obsolete 
rather than an opportunity.

### Page 9

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

Figure 5: Computation used to train notable AI systems (petaFLOP): log scale

Source: Epoch AI (citing estimates from AI literature), Our World in Data, Deutsche Bank Research

It is the robustness of this relationship that has made larger investments in 
compute capex a sine qua non for AI companies. 
However, this is not a law of physics and nothing guarantees it will continue . 
Indeed, in the ChatGPT era, five different approaches to scaling have been 
applied. Older strategies yield worse results, and new techniques, such as post -
training refinements, have been developed. The consensus amongst American 
and Chinese AI labs is that these will hold.  
While this may be a consensus amongst the commercial labs, there is scientific 
disagreement that apply ing scaling laws to today’s large -language models can 
achieve superintelligence. 
Yann LeCun, a Turing Award winner and formerly Meta's chief AI scientist, is blunt: 
"LLMs are not a path to superintelligence or even human-level intelligence”. Even 
inside the labs, the recipe is contested. Demis Hassabis of Google DeepMind has 
said he expects "one or two more big breakthroughs" to be needed "in addition to 
scaling". Ilya Sutskever, a co -founder and former chief scientist of OpenAI, has 
said the field is going "back to the age of research again, just with big computers". 
This is a scientific argument we are not in a position to evaluate , but for the 
purposes of our scenario, we will side with the labs’ position. 
For the moment, there is strong evidence that capabilities have in fact 
accelerated over the past three years, as reasoning models have outpaced non -
reasoning models by two or three times, according to Epoch data.

### Page 10

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 10

2.2 Recursive self-improvement: AI improving AI 
A cocktail-party definition of recursive self-improvement (RSI) is AI that improves 
itself. The reality is that it can be used in two ways: 
1. Self-improving agents that get better at using themselves: showing 
smarter use of prompts, tools, memory and workflows. The model -
building process is untouched. 
2. True RSI improves the process that builds the models: designing 
experiments, curating data, optimising algorithms and designing chips in 
a compounding loop. 
The former is like a factory worker who gets better at the job by learning the quirks 
of the machinery, getting organised and making fewer mistakes . The latter is like 
the worker redesigning the machine tools and then using those to build the next 
generation of machines. 
True RSI is what OpenAI’s chief scientist Jacob Pachoki referred to last month in 
a blog post about  “An Alien Mind ”. “Automated AI research is a more dramatic 
form of scaling intelligence with compute,” he wrote. “And of course as a part of 
it, AI will improve the computational substrate itself,” he added. Pachoki 
represents a broadly held consensus view amongst America n and Chinese labs 
that RSI is both possible and necessary for AI development. 
• OpenAI (June 2026): “AI doing AI research will become the determining 
factor of the pace of progress within the next few years.” 
• Google DeepMind’s Demis Hassabis (May 2026): “All the leading labs are 
quite focused on [RSI]…” Humanity is standing in the “foothills of the 
singularity”. 
• Alibaba CEO Eddie Wu (Sept 2026): RSI is “a concrete path forward,” with 
models autonomously designing experiments and driving a cycle of self-
evolution. 
For now, self -improving agents are the AI du jour. Noisy claims about the vast 
majority of coding being done by agents do not always stand up in the light of day 
from companies still hiring human coders at breakneck pace, albeit no longer to 
do the most mundane parts of the job.  
A better metric may be METR’s task -completion time horizon. This is measure of 
the complexity of software tasks that an AI agent can complete on its own, using 
the baseline of how long it would take a human expert to finish the task, at a 50 
percent success rate. So far the most advanced models have reached about 16 
hours, up from OpenAI’s GPT-4’s four minutes in March 2023.

### Page 11

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

Figure 6: Task horizon of software tasks that leading frontier models can complete 50% of the time (log scale)

Source: METR, Deutsche Bank Research

But software engineers can hold off on updating their resumes a little longer: 
there is a long road between 50 percent accuracy and the 99 percent or 100 
percent needed for serious enterprise use at scale, with the horizon potentially 
getting further away the nearer you get. Also METR notes that measurements 
above 16 hours are unreliable with the current task suite. As is the case across AI, 
the instrument is running out before the question is settled.  
More broadly, there are significant limitations to RSI:  
1. Research is clock-bound.  
• A frontier training run can take weeks on thousands of specialised chips, 
and researchers often cannot design the next experiment until the 
previous one has been completed.  
• That means that the speed of the loop is set by experiments completed 
per hour or day, not by ideas generated.  
• Compute is a hard ceiling that needs to be bought, buil t and connected 
to a grid. 
2. The real world defies verifiability.  
• The loop compounds only when the system can check its own output 
cheaply and automatically. 
• It is no coincidence that AI loves maths and coding. It is much harder to 
verify outputs over a long time horizon in the real world: does this drug 
work or does this strategy succeed?  
• RSI compounds fastest where the economy cares least and slows where 
the returns are largest.

### Page 12

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

3. Sequences are consequential.  
• Amdahl’s law finds that speed -up is capped when processes run in a 
sequence.  
• If you automate 90 percent, the residual 10 percent still limits you to a 
ten-times faster process. Nine women cannot produce a baby in one 
month.  
• The Usain Bolt of AI still has to run alongside the comparative tortoises 
of the training run itself, chip fabrication and real -world validation. Ten 
times faster is still not a regime break. 
This is borne out by METR’s series. Doubling every seven months is fast 
exponential growth but a take -off would show the doubling time itself – the 
second derivative – shrinking. Horizons are not (yet) exploding. 
Philosopher Toby Ord shows that recursive self -improvement can only reach a 
singularity if each cycle of improvement takes less time than the last, shrinking 
fast towards zero. He argues that real feedback loops have a floor: chips take time 
to make and models take time to train. A I progress could still outrun an 
exponential, but it would not go vertical. 
2.3 Scaling plus RSI: a match made in the race to AGI? 
No scaling and RSI means no short timeline for AGI. Without scaling, more 
compute buys nothing and capital is wasted. Without RSI, AI capability will 
continue to improve at the pace of flesh -and-blood human researchers, 
augmented by AI tools but not replac ed. Only with both will it be possible to 
produce a system that improves faster than the humans building it. Without one 
or the other, no quantity of chips, energy or capital will fix it. 
3. Can we get there practically? 
3.1 Infrastructure, chips and energy 
There is no consensus on the amount of training required to reach AGI. Estimates 
suggest that training 2026's top models required between 10^26 and 10^27 
FLOPs. If we hypothesise that an "AGI -like model" would require three orders of 
magnitude more training, this would require 10^30 FLOPs.   
Consider today’s US AI infrastructure, which runs to approximately 10^22 FLOPs. 
If all of this was conceivably used for a 10^30 FLOP training run, it would take 
nearly four years to complete.  
The physical infrastructure to practically support such a training run would 
require enormous power, perhaps around 6GW, about 50% more than the four 
Vogtle nuclear power plants recently activated in the US. The cost of the 
compute infrastructure would run to between $300bn and $500bn.  
Today’s advanced chips would simply not suffice. Back-of-the-envelope maths 
suggests that even using Nvidia’s most advanced Vera Rubin chips, training such 
a model in four months would require 7.8m chips and 26GW of facility power. 
That would cost $900bn today and a rental equivalent cost of $90bn.  
Of course, frontier compute is growing rapidly, and price -performance is 
declining. If current trends hold, a 2030 -era cluster capable of such a run would

### Page 13

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 13

need perhaps $200bn in computer and power hardware for a training run costing 
about $20bn.

Figure 7: Technology cost index over time (years since start of price decline)

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

The hyperscalers are leading the charge towards AGI. Alphabet, Amazon, Meta, 
Microsoft and Oracle are set to invest almost $800bn in capital expenditure this 
year, mostly for ever more powerful data centres, and over $1 trillion a year 
through 2030 and beyond.

Figure 8: Hyperscaler capital expenditure (including estimates)

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 14

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 14

By 2030, assuming no market retrenchment, this means additional $4 trillion to 
$6 trillion on AI compute capability invested. This infrastructure could 
accommodate a training run of 10^30 FLOPs with some comfort.  
But even if AI labs crack the secret sauce of RSI, and their finance chiefs are willing 
to invest in such large training runs, and hyperscalers are prepared to continue to 
build the infrastructure, the infrastructure needs to be built.  
Grid connection queues, turbine lead times and transmission build are measured 
in years, not the months it takes to assemble and air-freight the latest Nvidia chip. 
The lead time for substation transformers is currently as long as three years. 
Politics in the US has shifted towards slowing the pace of the AI build out and this 
force will run against the demands for larger data centres that would be essential 
for RSI.  
3.2 Capital 
Financing for AI has been abundant in the historic buildout of the past three years. 
However, it is still largely conditional not on revenue but on belief in a future 
payoff. 
RSI is a “key part of the investment thesis” in the “biggest scientific bet civilisation 
has ever made,” Google DeepMind’s chief strategy officer Jasjeet Sekhon said at 
a conference in August, The Information reported. “We have this danger we could 
hit an AI air pocket such that the expenditures happen but the revenues don’t 
show up.” 
An inflection point is approaching where the nature of investment changes from 
free cash flow and equity to debt, with all that entails in terms of exposure to 
rising rates and creditor appetite. The hyperscalers are reaching a point where 
capex is no long er covered by operating cashflow and where net debt exceeds 
earnings before interest, depreciation and amortisation (EBITDA). 
Alphabet, for example, posted its first quarterly free  cash outflow in nearly two 
decades in its most recent quarter, even as it lifted its full-year capex guidance to 
$205bn. It said it would continue to rent third -party capacity and reported a 
backlog of $514bn in contracted, not-yet-recognised data centre revenue. 
3.3 Data 
Data has been the new oil for some time now. But unfortunately for AI 
development it seems to be running out even faster. 
The effective stock of quality - and repetition -adjusted public human text was 
about 300 trillion tokens, with a vast 90 percent confidence interval of 100 trillion 
to 1,000 trillion, Epoch estimated in 2024. A token is roughly equivalent to three 
quarters of a word. By that count it was likely to be fully used between 2026 and 
2032.

### Page 15

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 15

Figure 9: Projections of stock of public text and data usage

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

However, since then, rather than data dropping off a cliff, there has been a rise in 
synthetic data generation, transfer learning from data-rich domains such as video 
and telemetry, and data efficiency. While the data shortage has not been 
resolved, the immediate crisis has been averted. 
AI now makes much of its own training data. When DeepMind's AlphaGo became 
superhuman at playing the mind-bendingly complex board game Go, it did so not 
by playing human experts, but by playing millions and millions of games against 
itself. 
For many domains relating to coding and mathematical proofs where automatic 
checks are possible, such synthetic experience can move things forward.  That is 
the case with AI research, where the data may be the results of experiments, all 
of which are bought by compute. About 90% of OpenAI's 2024 research compute 
went on such experiments. 
And increasingly, people write the tests rather than the answers. By the summer 
of 2026, selling training data for practi ce tasks was a nearly $9bn revenue 
business. Firms such as Mercor hire experts to generate long tasks against which 
the AI can be trained. 
The challenge here is that trust in the checker becomes a scarce input.

### Page 16

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 16

As AI makes production cheaper, it also makes establishing that production is 
accurate or reliable more expensive.  Domains which can be checked 
mechanically, such as mathematics, will be opened up more quickly. But those 
which rely on expert verification, such as medicine, engineering or law, may 
progress more slowly. 
The likely implications are twofold: a slower rate of diffusion into economic 
domains that are less verifiable and a larger share of production costs devoted to 
assurance. 
Another issue is that s ystems that have to  grade themselves learn to game the 
process. T his was partially what happened when an OpenAI model escap ed a 
sandbox and hacked into a third-party website. Trust replaced data as a wall. 
There are also question marks over the quality of data that is recycled repeatedly 
through the system. Although humans continue to produce original content, half 
of internet articles are now primarily generated by AI, Graphite data show.

Figure 10: Human vs primarily AI-generated articles on the internet

Source: Graphite (citing 55.4k articles from Common Crawl examined by AI detectors), Deutsche Bank Research

3.4 An incomplete case 
This analysis shows that the San Francisco Consensus, which emphasises RSI and 
a short timeline to AGI, is neither predestined nor impossible.  
Rather it depends on whether the science is right, on whether scaling is all you 
need, and on whether the infrastructure and capital required for that scaling is 
deployed and accessible to the leading labs.  
There is, in other words, a possible rather than impossible, path to AGI, subject to 
those conditions.  
None of this proves that superintelligence will ever arrive. A buildup at this scale 
has few peacetime precedents and vastly exceeds the likes of the Manhattan 
Project and Apollo programme.

### Page 17

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 17

It is uncertain whether there will be enough demand to pay upfront for the 
investment in achieving RSI that would justify that investment. And whether the 
friction of verification, and inevitable declining marginal returns, act as a drag on 
what otherwise might be a runaway process. 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
4.1 Separating AI progress from superintelligence 
So what if that thesis is wrong? Does the AI trade depend on a journey to AGI?  
AI can remain economically important even before RSI coupled with scaling laws 
lead to super intelligence . Companies do not need to wait for nebulous 
superintelligence to generate identifiable returns from AI in the medium term as 
they fold it into their systems, streamline workflows and roll out new business 
models. 
Investors already have exposure to RSI as a capital allocation because the leading 
US labs say RSI is a key part of their product strategy. And it is these labs whose 
products are catalysing and amplifying the infrastructure boom. 
Even r apid competitive diffusion does not mean every part of the physical 
economy changes instantly. 
The smartest superintelligence might identify the optimal buyout structure 
before breakfast, but it cannot force a financially constrained buyer to use it or 
persuade the seller to accept less than they have been expecting. Those 
challenges will be even more intractable where the parties are more complex, say, 
fiscally constrained sovereigns or foreign equity holders. 
4.2 Why the base case is attractive 
Superintelligence would be a chaotic accelerant to human society, and a 
potential regime break. But it is not the only scenario in which today’s investment 
in AI infrastructure makes economic sense. 
Adoption of AI tools by businesses is progressing approximately three times faster 
than the mobile, internet and cloud computing waves. From sector revenues 
below $1bn in 2022, the AI economy, excluding China, will likely exceed $200bn 
in revenues in 2026.  
Early winners are the cloud businesses. Google Cloud revenue jumped 82 percent 
from a year earlier in the most recent quarter as companies turned AI pilots into 
workflows. Amazon Web Services sales were up 37 percent and Microsoft’s AI 
business more than doubled. 
There is also slow, but increasing, evidence of a productivity uplift. The J-curve 
cuts both ways: front -loading investments is taking one step back to take two 
steps forward. The so -called complementary intangible investment that is 
(mis)measured as cost today to the detriment of the productivity statistics will 
flatter them in the years to come as it pays off. The base case does not require a 
regime break to show up as a productivity surprise, just for the accounting to 
catch up. 
The economic gains from AI that is less powerful than the AGI mooted by the labs 
would be substantial. And in this base case, they may also be attractive.

### Page 18

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 18

Hyperscaler capex is driving earnings at beneficiaries ranging from 
semiconductor makers to construction companies and accounting for between 
one-third and one -half of current US growth. The wealth effect of rising stock 
prices, with  the ratio of  US equity wealth to income rising from around 1.8 to 
almost 2.5 over the past three years, is supporting consumer spending. 
AI FOMO (fear of missing out) is buoying enterprise spending on cloud services 
and the groundwork for AI initiatives. Those are beginning to bear fruit in terms of 
personal productivity, cost savings and more targeted products and services. 
4.3 Investor watchpoints 
The arrival of ASI would be a disruptive economic shock. Investors need to be alert 
for the early signals. 
Rather than relying on a single probability forecast, investors should watch five 
indicators particularly closely for signs that AGI is nearing.  
1. AI speeds up AI 
• Track METR’s estimate of how much AI speeds up AI. Currently the 
capabilities of models are doubling roughly every three months. In a 
world of RSI, the pace of this speed-up will itself likely accelerate. 
2. AI can complete whole jobs 
• Track via the Remote Labor Index, a multi-sector benchmark. Currently it 
finds that the highest performing agent still only manages to automate 
20 percent of the real-world, economically valuable freelance projects it 
attempts. 
3. Compute remains scarce 
• On a path to superintelligence, demand for compute will continue to 
outrun supply. Track GPU one-year rental prices, up by about a third over 
the past year. 
4. Firms bifurcate 
• Leaders and laggards in the same industry diverge while averages stay 
the same. Look at revenue per employee in top - and bottom -quartile 
firms 
5. Junior hiring comes under significant pressure 
• … And then the pressure rises up the ranks.

排。

## Document 326: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

## Document 351: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.

Next, the direction of travel on the 
contribution of SOE dividends from the 
investment agency to the state coffers 
for 2027 remains unclear, after being 
accounted for this year. Lastly, 
successive stimulus measures in-kind 
and wage subsidies were announced this 
year and could marginally add to the 
strain in 2027 (0.1-0.2% of GDP).

Discussions around the State Finance 
Law also warrant a watch. We maintain 
our deficit forecast at a wider -2.8% to -
2.9% range to account for any revenue 
shortfall or a gradual pace of growth. 
Nonetheless, deficits/debt are likely to 
stay below key thresholds  and not 
emerge as a flashpoint for the markets.

MONETARY POLICY ON AN EVEN 
KEEL

Bank Indonesia has left the benchmark 
rate unchanged  since July 2026, after 
100bp hikes in 1H26. While tighter global 
financial conditions and geopolitical 
risks were key risks on the horizon, the BI 
held the domestic GDP forecast at 4.9 -
5.7% (DBSf: 5.3%) alongside 
manageable inflation, notwithstanding 
El Nino-related risks. Inflation rose to 3.3% 
yoy in Sep26, towards the high end of the 
target range. Authorities have, however, 
assured that the impact of higher food 
prices due to poor weather will be 
addressed through administrative 
measures by regional bodies.

Contained inflation expectations ha ve 
reduced the urgency for BI to tighten 
policy further, allowing policymakers to 
prioritise growth and financial stability 
considerations. The emphasis on 
strengthening the balance of payments 
position reflects continued caution over 
external financing conditions and capital 
flows. However, with the rupiah 
expected to remain stable on the back of 
policy measures and fundamenta ls, the 
BI did not view current external 
pressures as severe enough to warrant 
additional tightening.

BI introduced a host of measures at the 
June to September policy meetings , 
including FX monetary hedging 
instruments and a mechanism to provide 
foreign investors with greater flexibility 
in managing IDR-denominated 
investments in Indonesia.  
These included –  
a) swap hedging per tenor bucket was 
increased to 3M - 15%; 6M - 20%; 12M - 
25%, from 12.5% earlier.  
b) DNDF incentive was increased to 6M - 
25% and 12M – 30%. An increase in FX/ 
NDF swap premium incentives for longer 
tenors will lower hedging costs and

### Page 5

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

encourage longer tenor positions, in turn 
increasing longevity of these flows;  
c) Launch of the VASTRA scheme (FX 
Market Transactions for Hedging 
through Partner Banks) got underway, 
with the naming of 14 domestic partner 
banks. This facility is designed to help 
investors transact with lower underlying 
documentation requirements, tap into 
longer banking hours , and access 
onshore hedging facilities.  
d) deepen FX and money markets by 
expanding Local Currency Transactions 
(LCT) between IDR and CNY , besides a 
few other currencies.

Policymakers are likely to tap a 
combination of administrative and 
macroprudential measures rather than 
solely interest rates to defend the 
currency as well as keep inflationary 
expectations in check.

We expect the pause on rates to extend 
into 4Q26.  This call might be at risk if 
depreciation pressures on the rupiah 
mount again on unfavourable global 
developments and if the US Fed 
undertakes a more aggressive hik ing 
cycle than our baseline (of 75bp hikes).

WIDER CAD WILL BE PARTLY 
OFFSET BY POSITIVE FLOWS

Indonesia’s 1H26 current account 
deficit (CAD) widened significantly  to -
$16bn vs -$3bn in the comparable period 
a year ago. Much of this deterioration 
was led by a narrower goods trade 
balance, while  the services deficit 
widened on the seasonal travel account 
due to the Hajj period. Higher coupon 
payments weighed on the primary 
account.  This pressure is likely to persist 
in 2H as the oil & gas trade deficit 
remains wide and export performance 
has been fi rm but tracking lower than 
regional peers, likely also impacted by 
the El Nino phenomenon in key agri 
commodities. We expect full year CAD to 
be closer to 1.8-2% of GDP from -0.1% in 
2025.

Much rests on the capital flows account. 
While 1H balance of payments (BOP) also 
widened, there was palpable 
improvement in 2Q26 vs 1Q.  2Q BOP 
narrowed significantly to $0.9bn from a 
wide $9.1bn in 1Q, helped by positive 
capital account flows – primarily 
stronger portfolio investment on SRBI 
and debt inflows, and net FDI position. 
This dependency is likely to extend to 2H, 
with capital fl ows till Sep26 holding up 
on account of hedging discounts and 
moves to improve inflows. Global 
conditions, including the narrow ID -US 
yields, however, warrant attention. 
Nonetheless, full-year BOP could remain

### Page 6

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

wide, on a scale comparable to the 2025 
gap.

IN SUMMARY

IDR markets have been relatively 
steady in October despite higher oil 
prices and a bid dollar , though 
sentiment remains sensitive to external 
developments and swings in global risk 
appetite. USDIDR traded within 17600 -
18000, while 10Y yield hovered around 
7.15-7.25%. A jump in the Aug goods 
trade surplus aided this respite, 
alongside foreign purchases of domestic 
debt, which have remained positive this 
month and on a  YTD basis. Insurers, the 
central bank, and retail investors have 
remained key anchors for the bond 
market. SRBI rates are off highs to sub -
7% levels but are attracting steady 
demand, likely encouraged by hedging 
discounts. An improved outlook for flows 
has helped contain spillovers from a 
wider current account deficit. This does 
not take away from the fact that the 
rupiah is amongst the worst performers 
on a year-to-date basis.

Separately, markets will also monitor 
developments in the political economy, 
institutional architecture, and formation, 
as well as the role of state -led agencies 
(e.g., DSI) and progress on the 
centralisation of decision -making. We 
expect the BI to keep on hold, however, 
pressure to tighten policy might return if 
the US Fed hikes rates well into 2027 
and the US dollar benefits from a risk-off 
environment, weighing on the currency.

## Document 388: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get
longer
August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.

## Document 475: 【國泰證期研究部】網通產業-調變未定通道先行400G電路將到來-20261029

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】網通產業-調變未定通道先行400G電路將到來-20261029.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 
產業更新-網通產業 
調變未定通道先行，400G SerDes 將到來 
What’s New – 規格已定，訊號編碼方式未決 
資料中心交換器的埠速每隔幾年翻倍，方法是把每條線路的速度加倍，下世代的3.2T 需 8 條線路、
每條 400G，也就是 400G SerDes，即光互連論壇(Optical Internetworking Forum, OIF) 的 CEI-
448G。 該規格的框架文件已於2025 年 11 月發布 ，2026 年 2 月並啟動極短距與長距兩項子規格設
定，但這代首次遇到既有線路無法負荷，沿用現行PAM4 編碼需要線路承受 112GHz 頻率，但受連
接器限制只有約 90GHz，雖然沒有明言 PAM4 無法運作；但改用 PAM6 編碼可降到約 87GHz，代
價是訊號品質下降，且光訊號那端仍是 PAM4 須多一層轉換。兩者差距不只在頻率，階數越多則每
階間距越窄，糾錯負擔與晶片複雜度同步上升，關鍵在連接器能否再往上突破一成以上的頻寬，而
不在編碼技術本身是否有新解。 
Trends/Outlook – 三種線路方案同時出現 
速度越快 ， 銅線能傳的距離越短，標準規格的 112G 世代還有 2 公尺 、224G 世代降到 1 公尺 ，400G
世代連目標都未訂，除非以主動銅纜或繞過電路板的纜線設計可再延長。距離縮短迫使光學元件往
晶片旁移動，規格要求把晶片與到鄰近光引擎 的距離定在約 150mm，這正是近封裝光學 (Near-
Packaged Optics, NPO)的定義，意即 400G 電路就是 NPO 出現的原因，產業因而分三種方案。一
是拉高線路速度並換連接器以延續可插拔，NVIDIA 與思科的 USFP 對上 Arista 與 MSA 的 XPO，
前者單模組容量 12.8Tbps、頻寬與前面板密度為現行 4 倍，後者的多源協議成員半年內由 25 家增
至逾 150 家；二是把光學搬到晶片旁或封裝內的 NPO 與共同封裝光學(CPO)；三是 Credo 的寬而
慢，以數百條慢速光通道取代少數高速通道。 
Investment Summary 
1、 400G SerDes 的導入時程將呈現晶片先到、周邊生態系後到 的狀態，預期 博通支援 400G 
SerDes 的交換器晶片 TH7 將有機會於 1H27 問世，28 年則有配合 TH7 的第 4 代 CPO 模組
推出，乙太網聯盟的次世代晶片同樣列於 28 年，主機端的 PCIe Gen8 規格亦預計 28 年才完
成。224G/1.6T 或 224G/800G 仍是 26 與 27 年各廠商的主要貢獻獲利來源；未來 448G 的
瓶頸不全是因為晶片本身，尚有連接器、基板材料與主機端介面等週邊生態系需完備。 
2、 對台廠而言，將來為了導入 400G SerDes 架構，無論選擇 USFP 或 XPO，交換器前面板、連
接器與液冷模組都須重新設計，與晶片商的整合能力更為重要，同時可滿足 CSP 客戶在電訊
號與光通訊的需求，具 Tomahawk 平台開發能力的智邦 (2345 TT) 兩者皆可承接；貿聯 -
KY(3665 TT)既發表電訊號的 448G 互連方案，又是 Credo 寬而慢生態系整合商，為唯一同時
站上兩條路線的台廠。目前瓶頸落在被動零件與材料，受惠順序應由連接器、線材與高階基板
材料端向週邊網卡與交換器擴散。

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/29

2 
訊號編碼與線路條件的取捨 
單一通道傳輸速度要提高，通常往兩種方向發展，一是每秒送出更多次訊號，二是每一次訊號多
帶幾個位元 。 早期乙太網到每通道25G 至28G 為止採用不歸零編碼(Non-Return-to-Zero, NRZ)，
訊號只有高低兩種狀態、一次帶一個位元；從 56G 世代起改用四階脈衝振幅調變 (Pulse 
Amplitude Modulation 4-level, PAM4)，把訊號分成四個高度、一次帶兩個位元，送出次數因而
減半，這正是過去三個世代能沿用既有線路的原因。到了 400G 世代，PAM4 與 PAM6 的差異如
表一與圖一所示，階數越多，每秒送出的次數與線路要承受的頻率越低，但每一階之間的間距也
越窄、越容易判讀錯誤。PAM6 還多一個麻煩，六不是二的整數倍，必須用兩次訊號傳五個位元
的方式折衷，編解碼複雜度明顯高於 PAM4，這也是它遲遲無法定案的原因之一。 
表一、NRZ、PAM4 與 PAM6 三種訊號編碼方式比較 
比較項目 NRZ (PAM2) PAM4 PAM6 
電壓準位數 2 4 6 
每符號位元數 1 2 log₂6≈2.585(實作 2.5) 
448Gbps 下符號率 448 GBd 224 GBd 173.31 GBd 
448Gbps 下奈奎斯特頻率 224 GHz 112 GHz 86.66 GHz 
相對 PAM4 之 SNR 差(BER 1e-5) 約+9.5dB(理論) 0dB(基準) -3.62dB

與光域 IMDD PAM4 對齊 否 是 否(須 6 對 4 gearbox)

主要應用世代 ≤28G/通道 56G~224G/通道 448G 候選 
資料來源：OIF-FD-CEI-448G-01.0、OIF 448G AI Workshop，國泰證期研究部整理 
圖一、NRZ / PAM4 / PAM6 Signalling at 448 Gbps

資料來源：OIF-FD-CEI-448G-01.0，國泰證期研究部 
編碼方式最後是由線路條件決定，OIF 指出目前 448G 線路的頻寬上限只有約 90GHz，主要卡在
連接器；晶片封裝端反而不是問題，封裝接腳間距1.0mm 對應 58GHz、0.65mm 對應 90GHz、
0.5mm 對應 115GHz，而0.5mm 以下已可量產 ， 意即封裝本身已能支撐PAM4 所需的 112GHz，
真正的瓶頸在連接器與各段轉接。訊號衰減的要求也同步收緊，56G 世代允許 30dB、224G 世代
為 40dB，400G 世代則要在更高的頻率下仍維持 40dB 以上，連帶使高階基板材料與連接器成為
能否成案的關鍵，而非晶片設計本身。表二也列出各級介面的耗電目標，從最短距離的 0.5 pJ/b
到最長的 2.5 pJ/b，距離越遠越耗電，這也說明縮短電的傳輸距離本身就是省電手段，並且是後
面評估寬而慢路線時應有的比較基準。

資料來源：OIF、Ethernet Alliance TEF 2025，國泰證期研究部整理 
訊號品質不足的部分可以靠糾錯編碼 (Forward Error Correction, FEC) 補回來，但能補的幅度有
限。OIF 明言現行的糾錯能力已不足以支撐 448G，必須再加一層額外編碼。華為在 OIF 研討會
提出的比較顯示，把糾錯能力從 6.9dB 提升到 7.8dB，代價是額外負擔由 5.8%升至 12.1%、運
算複雜度增加近五倍；而 PAM6 本身就需要約 12%的額外負擔，等於在起跑點就先付出一筆成
本。換句話說，PAM6 在頻率上省下來的，有相當一部分會在糾錯這項功能還回去。 
從晶片實作來看，PAM4 要做到 400G，類比電路的頻寬必須加倍、雜訊與抖動要改善一倍，製
程得推進到 2 奈米以下；PAM6 雖因送出次數較少而在部分電路上較省，但等化與判讀電路的複
雜度分別增加約五成與一倍 ， 綜合下來只略優於PAM4， 省下的電力大致剛好用在額外的糾錯上 。
結論是電與光最好維持同一種編碼，以免每種架構都要多一層轉換；PAM6 的技術風險較低，但
PAM4 在光的那一端與抗干擾上明顯較好，最終要由模組、線性驅動與銅纜三類設計共同決定。
值得注意的是，OIF 已明確表示業界對折衷速率並無需求，意即 400G 電路沒有中間站，產業必
須一次跨過這道門檻，沒有緩衝的速度可選，採用時程因此格外嚴謹。 
USFP、XPO 與寬而慢 
既然上限來自連接器，400G 電路的架構分流實際上由連接器進度決定。Amphenol 在 OCP 峰會
把 224G 之後的瓶頸依序排為晶片封裝出線第一、可插拔介面第二；TE Connectivity 則表示現行
OSFP 規格沒有通往 448G 的明確路徑。這也解釋了標準目標與實際系統的落差：規格中的 1 公
尺，是電路板走線、兩個連接器與纜線合計落在約 40dB 損耗預算內的結果；若把纜線直接接到
封裝、繞過電路板出線，同樣的預算就能讓給纜線本身。NVIDIA 的 NVL72 機櫃即屬此類，背板
以逾 5,000 條銅纜完成櫃內互連，總長超過 2 英里，而單條長度仍在 1 公尺以內，並未脫離同一
量級 。 四家主要供應商提出兩類互不相容的解法 ：Amphenol 與鴻騰精密(Foxconn Interconnect 
Technology, FIT)保留現行的卡緣(Card edge)接法，靠收緊公差與縮小接點間距克服；TE 與立訊
(Luxshare)則改用兩件式接頭，以金屬外框把每一對訊號完全包覆。四種設計的差別，最後體現
在頻寬能撐到多高，也就是能否達到 PAM4 所需的 112GHz，這道門檻決定了整體架構的走向，
也決定了哪些供應商進得了這一代的名單，時間壓力因此落在被動零件端。 
表三、OCP 2026 各廠 448G 可插拔連接器方案比較 
方案/廠商 介面型式 頻寬 超出 PAM4(112GHz)餘裕 差動阻抗 資料性質 
224G 可插拔(Amphenol 範例) 卡緣 約 78 GHz 不足 約 85Ω 已量產出貨 
XPO 448G(Amphenol) 卡緣、公差收緊 約 123 GHz 約 10% 89~92Ω 推估曲線、展場 demo 
XPO 448G(鴻騰精密 FIT) 卡緣、新接點 約 118 GHz 約 5% 89~92Ω 模擬，原型待實測 
MegaLane(TE Connectivity) 兩件式、無殘段 約 126 GHz 約 13% 約 92Ω 模擬，TE 稱已有實測 
448G OSFP(立訊 Luxshare) 兩件式 約 111 GHz 幾近於零 92Ω 與 85Ω 併行 模擬，已有樣品 
資料來源：OCP Global Summit 2026 各廠簡報，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/29

4 
如表三所示，四個 448G 設計都能跨過 PAM6 所需的約 90GHz，但相對 PAM4 所需的 112GHz
餘裕只有 0 至 14GHz。換言之，PAM4 能否成立幾乎完全押在連接器能否再往上推一成以上，這
也是 OIF 遲遲不敢定案的原因；Amphenol 甚至為了達到 120 至 130GHz，XPO 可能必須犧牲
與舊規格的相容性。400G SerDes 的編碼，本質上不是訊號處理技術之爭，而是連接器這類被動
零件的機構與公差之爭，勝負取決於幾家連接器廠的製造精度。 
NVIDIA 與思科 2026 年 10 月 12 日共同發表的超高密度小型可插拔(Ultra-Dense Small Form 
Factor Pluggable, USFP)，是PAM4 路線的第一個商業化產品。 這是首款支援每條線路400Gbps
的模組，在與現行 OSFP 相同的外形內放進 16 條雙向通道、單模組容量達 12.8Tbps，未來可再
擴充到 32 條以上；相較目前每條 200Gbps 的模組，頻寬與前面板密度提升為四倍、能源效率約
1.3 倍，並可在不另外配置散熱迴路的條件下支援最高 100 瓦；相較之下，現行 1.6T OSFP 世代
單模組功耗約 40 瓦，卻已須外掛液冷籠座與冷板組件散熱，如圖四，USFP 等於把散熱責任收回
模組本身。兩家公司明言其光學設計是以簡化訊號處理來降低延遲與耗電，方向指向線性驅動可
插拔光模組(Linear Pluggable Optics, LPO) ，而 LPO 唯一可行的編碼正是 PAM4。生態系已與
TE Connectivity、Amphenol 合作機殼與連接器，並計畫以開放規格推出。值得注意的是 TE 表
示 USFP 採用其 MegaLane 接頭，正是四種方案中頻寬餘裕最高的兩件式設計，顯示NVIDIA 與
思科選擇以更換接頭型式來換取 PAM4 的可行性，而非在編碼上退讓，等於把這一代是否能導入
的成敗因素交給連接器供應商的製造精度。 
圖二、XPO (eXtra-dense Pluggable Optics) Connector, Cage and Plug Module 
資料來源：鴻騰精密(FIT)，國泰證期研究部整理 
圖三、USFP 12.8Tb/s per module        圖四、Current 1.6T OSFP liquid-cooled 2X8 cage

### Page 5

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/29

5 
與 USFP(Ultra-Dense Small Form Factor Pluggable) 正面競爭的是 Arista 主導、鴻騰精密與
Amphenol 同屬卡緣陣營的超高密度可插拔光學(eXtra-Dense Pluggable Optics, XPO)。該協議
2026 年 3 月成立時約 25 家成員，至 OCP 峰會已逾 150 家；Arista 揭露的模組規格為單模組
12.8Tbps、整合冷板可散熱至每模組 400 瓦，與 USFP 同屬 12.8Tbps 等級；Amphenol 揭露的
連接器規格則涵蓋 64 條高速通道、單一48V 供電、整合液冷並相容MPO-16 光介面。兩套方案
的根本差異在於，XPO 保留現行卡緣接法以沿用既有機構與散熱設計，改版責任落在模組端的小
電路板上；USFP 則接受更換接頭型式，以換取更高的頻寬餘裕。對台廠而言，無論最後收斂到哪
一方，前面板、機殼、連接器與液冷模組都必須重新設計，交換器的工程價值反而因此提高，具
Tomahawk 平台開發能力的智邦(2345 TT) 在兩套規格下都能承接設計與製造，不必在規格定案
前押注任何單一陣營。 
第三條路線乾脆不參加速度競賽，Credo 在 OCP 峰會以 Wider. Slower. Better.為題主張，光學
應該在可靠度、耗電與成本這三項原本屬於銅纜的強項上取勝，作法是用數百條慢速的微型發光
元件取代少數高速通道，把加速壓力留在電的那一端。其耗電論據是：銅纜加上晶片電路約需
5pJ/bit，微型發光元件則低於 2pJ/bit，其中紅光微型 LED 低於 1pJ/bit、可達 2 公尺，藍光低
於 2pJ/bit、可達 10 公尺，微型雷射低於 2pJ/bit、可達 50 公尺。不過這個比較的基準是銅纜方
案；若改與表二中 OIF 對線性介面訂的 2.5 pJ/b 目標相比，優勢會明顯收斂。以 NVIDIA VR200 
NVL72 機櫃估算，銅纜方案約耗 10kW、約佔整櫃用電 5%，改用微型發光元件約可降到 4kW、
省下約六成 ， 相當於每顆GPU 省 80 瓦 。 產品方面 ，Credo 已於 10 月 12 日發表 1.6T 主動光纜 ，
以約 200 條光纖承載、單端耗電約 12 瓦、可達 50 公尺，刻意採用最簡單的 NRZ 編碼而不使用
訊號處理晶片 ， 並內建備援通道 ， 預計2027 年量產 ； 其路線圖為2027 年 1.6T 光纜與 6.4T NPO、
2028 至 2029 年 3.2T 光纜與 CPO。生態系中發光元件由 ams OSRAM 供應，光纖與連接器涵
蓋康寧(Corning)等廠，整合商則包含 Credo、貿聯(BizLink)與正崴(Foxlink)，三家整合商中台廠
即占兩席，是寬而慢路線上最直接的台廠受惠位置，惟兩家目前皆屬組裝與整合角色，尚未切入
上游的發光與光學元件，單位價值相對有限。 
圖五、µEmitter-Based Optical Interconnect Roadmap 
資料來源：Credo，國泰證期研究部整理 
須強調三條路線並非互斥，而是對應不同的傳輸距離。規格定義的距離由短到長依序為：封裝內
晶片對晶片約 25mm、晶片到封裝內光引擎約 50mm、晶片到鄰近光引擎約 150mm、晶片到可
插拔模組約 250mm、同一塊電路板內約 50cm、跨板或跨機箱約 1 公尺。其中 150mm 這一級
正是 NPO 的定義，而120mm 的線性級別明確指定使用 PAM4。也就是說，400G 電路在壓縮銅
線可達距離的同時，等於直接在規格裡為 NPO 與線性驅動留下位置，預期 NPO 也將於 27 年底
至 28 年進入市場，貿聯-KY(3665 TT)則同時出現在 448G 高速互連與寬而慢這兩條路線上。

### Page 6

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/29

6 
主機端同步倍速，PCIe 世代為何決定網卡速度 
400G 電路的時程之所以能相對確定地落在 2028 年之後 ， 還有一個主機端的原因 。 網卡的工作是
把線路上收到的每一個位元搬進主機記憶體 ， 反之亦然 ， 因此主機介面的頻寬不能低於網路埠速 。
主機介面就是 PCIe，它的世代因而與乙太網埠速形成一對一對應，背後邏輯與前面的線路完全相
同：每一代把每條線路的速度加倍，也在同一代遇上編碼方式的轉折。以業界標準的 x16 插槽計
算，PCIe Gen5 每條線路 32GT/s、單向有效頻寬約 505Gbps，承載 400G 埠速還有 1.26 倍餘
裕；但面對 800G 只剩 0.63 倍、短缺約 37%。這不是設計餘裕不夠，而是結構性不足，這正是
Gen5 只能支撐到 400G 的原因，與設計優劣無關。 
表四、PCIe 世代與可承載乙太網埠速對應關係 
PCIe 世代 每通道速率 調變與編碼 x16 單向有效頻寬 可承載埠速 頻寬餘裕 代表網卡 規格發布 
Gen4 16 GT/s NRZ、128b/130b 約 252 Gbps 200G 約 1.26 倍 — 2017 年 
Gen5 32 GT/s NRZ、128b/130b 約 505 Gbps 400G 約 1.26 倍 ConnectX-7 2019 年 
Gen6 64 GT/s PAM4、1b/1b+FLIT+FEC 約 968 Gbps 800G 約 1.21 倍 Thor Ultra 2022 年 
Gen7 128 GT/s PAM4、1b/1b+FLIT+FEC 約 1,936 Gbps 1.6T 約 1.21 倍 — 2025 年 6 月 
Gen8 256 GT/s 待定 約 3,872 Gbps(雙向 1TB/s) 3.2T 約 1.21 倍 — 
2025 年宣布
預計 2028 年 
資料來源：PCI-SIG、Broadcom、AMD、Marvell，國泰證期研究部整理 
PCIe Gen6 把每條線路速度倍增到 64GT/s，並在同一代完成兩項與乙太網完全相同的轉折：編
碼由 NRZ 改為 PAM4， 並改用新的封包格式搭配糾錯 ，x16 單向有效頻寬因而提升到約968Gbps，
承載 800G 有 1.21 倍餘裕。這個對應關係可由實際產品驗證：博通 2025 年 10 月發表的業界首
款 800G AI 網卡 Thor Ultra，明確採用 PCIe Gen6 x16 主機介面；AMD 在 Helios 機櫃所列的
第二代 Pensando Vulcano 網卡同樣是 800G、 同樣走Gen6， 每顆GPU 配置三張 、 合計2.4Tbps。
兩款 800G 網卡都無法在 Gen5 上成立，也說明 800G 網卡必須與 Gen6 同時問世，兩者的時程
實為同一條，網卡無法在主機介面到位前先行推出。 
依同樣的倍增邏輯，PCIe Gen7 已於 2025 年 6 月發布規格，每條線路 128GT/s、x16 單向約
1,936Gbps，對應 1.6T 埠速同樣是 1.21 倍餘裕；Gen8 則規劃每條線路 256GT/s、x16 單向約
3,872Gbps、雙向約 1TB/s，對應的正是 3.2T 埠速，也就是400G 電路世代。Marvell 已於 2026
年 2 月在 DesignCon 展示運行於 256GT/s 的 PCIe 8.0 電路——在規格尚未完成、系統尚未成
形的階段就先展示晶片，反映的正是主機介面與網路介面必須同代到位的結構性約束。值得注意
的是，PCIe 的線路要穿過插槽、轉接卡與較長的板上走線，條件比網路側更嚴苛，因此它的編碼
方式同樣還沒定案，面臨與 400G 電路相同性質的取捨。 
把三個層面合在一起看，400G SerDes 電路能否成立不取決於單一元件的突破，而是編碼方式、
連接器頻寬與主機介面三者必須同時到位：編碼的取捨懸在連接器能否再推進一成以上的頻寬，
連接器的兩種路線已分別由 Arista 主導的 XPO 與 NVIDIA、思科的 USFP 代表，主機端則要等
PCIe Gen8 規格於 2028 年完成 。 而三者的時程並不同步 ， 呈現的是晶片先到 、 周邊生態系後到。
依通路訪查，預期博通支援 400G SerDes 的交換器晶片 TH7 將有機會於 1H27 問世，28 年則有
配合 TH7 的第 4 代 CPO 模組推出，乙太網聯盟的次世代晶片同樣列於 28 年，主機端的 PCIe 
Gen8 規格亦預計 28 年才完成。224G/1.6T 或 224G/800G 仍是 26 與 27 年各廠商的主要貢獻
獲利來源；未來 448G 的瓶頸不全是因為晶片本身，尚有連接器、基板材料與主機端介面等週邊
生態系需完備。受惠順序因而由連接器、線材與高階基板材料端向週邊網卡與交換器擴散，對應
台廠為貿聯-KY(3665 TT)與智邦(2345 TT)。

World Trends

2026 年10 月 20 日

1/3 
ブラジル大統領選は決選投票を前に一段と接戦の様相をみせる 
～初の直接討論会は非難の応酬に終始、市場は選挙後の政策運営を巡る不透明感を警戒～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月25 日に実施される大統領選挙の決選投票を前に、10 月 18 日に初めてテ
レビ討論会が実施された。討論会では、ルラ大統領とフラビオ・ボルソナロ氏の間で汚職疑
惑を巡る非難の応酬が目立ち、具体的な政策論争は限定的となった。直近の世論調査におい
ては、両候補の支持率が接近しており、今後はテレビ討論会の影響に加え、支持層の投票参
加の動向が勝敗を左右するとみられる。経済政策の面では、社会保障や公共投資を重視する
ルラ氏と、歳出削減や民間主導の成長を志向するフラビオ氏の違いが鮮明となる一方、財政
健全化と景気下支えの両立は共通の課題となっている。金融市場においては、両候補の支持
率が接近するなど選挙情勢を巡る不透明感が意識されており、選挙後の財政運営や議会との
関係、対外政策の方向性がブラジル経済や金融市場を左右する重要な要因となろう。

【初の直接討論会では汚職疑惑を巡る応酬が目立ち、具体的な政策論争は限定的】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施予定の大統領選挙の決選投票を前に、選挙戦が最終局面を迎え
ている。10 月４日に実施された第１回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長男で右派ＰＬ（自由党）
から出馬したフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員が得票率 47.03％、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）から出馬し
た現職のルラ大統領が同 45.16％となり、いずれも過半数に届かなかった。このため、両候補によ
る決選投票が実施されることとなった（注１）。 こうしたなか、10 月 18 日には両者による初めての
テレビ討論会が開催された。 
討論会では、経済政策や汚職問題などが議題となったものの、双方による非難の応酬が目立ち、
具体的な政策を巡る議論は限定的なものとなった。 ルラ大統領は、過去３期にわたる政権運営を通
じた社会保障政策や低所得者層への支援策などの実績を強調した。一方で、フラビオ氏は現政権の
経済運営や汚職問題を批判し、政権交代の必要性を訴えた。特に、両候補に関連する汚職疑惑を巡
る応酬は激しく、ルラ氏が双方の資産・金融取引に関する情報開示を求める場面もみられた。こう
した展開は、ブラジル社会における政治的な分断の根深さを改めて浮き彫りにしたといえる。

注１ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
【直近の世論調査では両候補の支持率が接近、テレビ討論会の影響を見極める必要がある】 
一方、決選投票に向けた世論調査では、両候補の支持率が接近する動きがみられる。10 月 19 日
に公表された調査では、有効票ベースの支持率はフラビオ氏が 51.1％、ルラ大統領が 48.9％とな
った。10 月９日に公表された前回調査では、それぞれ 52.8％、47.2％であったため、両候補の差
は 5.6ptから 2.2ptに縮小した。さらに、同日に公表された別の調査では、フラビオ氏が 50.3％、
ルラ大統領が49.7％と、統計上の誤差を考慮すれば両候補が拮抗する結果となっている。 
ただし、 これらの調査はテレビ討論会の前に実施されたものであり、 討論会の内容が有権者の投
票行動に与えた影響を直接示すものではないことに注意する必要がある。今後公表される調査にお
いて、支持率の変化が確認されるかが注目される。第１回投票では、棄権率が 21.02％に達してお
り、 政治不信の広がりが有権者の投票参加に及ぼす影響も無視できない。なお、 世論調査によれば、
９割以上の有権者が投票先をすでに決めており、 投票先を変更するつもりはないと回答していると
される。このため、決選投票に向けては、明確な支持を示していない有権者の動向に加え、各候補
が支持層の投票参加をどこまで促すことができるかが重要になる。

【両候補の経済政策を巡る方向性には明確な違い、財政健全化と景気下支えの両立が課題に】 
経済政策を巡っては、両候補の方向性に明確な違いがみられる。ルラ氏は、社会保障政策の拡充
や公共投資を通じた所得再分配と景気下支えを重視しており、 現行の財政運営の枠組みを維持する
姿勢を示している。一方、フラビオ氏は、公的部門の縮小や歳出削減、税負担の軽減を通じた民間
主導の経済成長を志向している。10 月 18 日のテレビ討論会においても、ルラ氏は過去の政権運営
における経済成長や所得再分配の実績を強調したのに対して、フラビオ氏は生活費の上昇や家計債
務の増大、政府債務の拡大を問題視する発言を行っている。 
両候補ともに政府債務の増大に対処する必要性を認識している一方、 財政健全化の実現に向けた
具体的な手法には違いがある。 ルラ氏は経済成長による税収拡大と歳出管理を重視する一方、フラ
ビオ氏は新たな財政ルールの導入や行政経費の削減を打ち出している。とはいえ、 いずれの政策に
ついても、実現に際しては議会との調整が必要となるうえ、 歳出削減や税制改革などが経済活動に
及ぼす影響を見極める必要がある。このため、選挙後の経済政策を評価するうえでは、政策の方向
性だけでなく、財源の裏付けや議会における実現可能性が重要となる。

【金融市場では選挙情勢に加え、財政運営や対外政策を巡る不透明感が意識される展開】 
金融市場においては、 選挙結果によって財政政策や国営企業の経営方針、 対外政策などが変化す
る可能性が意識されている。第１回投票の直後には、フラビオ氏が首位となったことを受けて、通
貨レアル、主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）ともに大きく上昇するなど、右派政権の誕生を好感する
動きをみせた （注２） 。 しかし、 その後は両候補の支持率が拮抗している様子が確認されていること

注２ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
を受けて、ともに上値の重い展開となっ
ている （図１） 。また、 長期金利には上昇
圧力がかかる動きが確認されており、財
政運営やインフレ動向を巡る懸念が引き
続き意識される展開となっている。ただ
し、金融市場の動きには国際金融市場や
原油価格の変動なども影響しており、選
挙情勢のみを要因と捉えることは適切で
はない。 
さらに、対外政策を巡っては、両候補
の間で姿勢の違いが明確であり、その行
方が注目される。ルラ政権は、中国をはじめとする新興国との関係やBRICSの枠組みを重視してき
た。一方で、フラビオ氏は米トランプ政権との関係強化を志向する姿勢を強めている。このため、
選挙後の政権運営は、米中対立が続く国際環境において、ブラジルの貿易・投資関係にも影響を及
ぼす可能性が考えられる。とはいえ、中国はブラジルにとって重要な貿易相手国であること、ボル
ソナロ前政権下でも中国との貿易が拡大してきたことを勘案すれば、外交姿勢の変化が直ちに経済
関係の大幅な見直しにつながるとは限らないであろう。 
今後は、 決選投票までに公表される世論調査の動向に加え、 両候補が財政健全化に向けてどの程
度具体的な政策を示すかが注目される。また、10 月 25 日の投票結果が確定した後も、新政権の政
策運営や議会との関係を巡る不透明感が残る可能性がある。 ブラジル経済は、 財政の持続可能性を
確保しつつ、 物価の安定と経済成長を両立させるという課題に直面しており、 選挙後の政策運営が
金融市場の動向を左右する重要な要因となることが予想される。

3Q26毛利率下滑，受產品組合及消費性需求疲弱 ：公司於 2026年 10
月 30日舉行法說會，公告 3Q26財務數據，營收 669億元 (QoQ -5.7%，
YoY-4.3%)，毛利率 10.1%(QoQ -2.9ppts，YoY+0.5ppts)，營業淨利率
-3.5%(QoQ -3.8ppts，YoY-0.9ppts)，營業外利益 3.65 億元，單季 EPS 
-0.33元。3Q26  Display、Mobility 及 Vertical 業務營收占比分別為 46%、
30%、20%，主要衰退來自 Display 業務，因品牌廠上半年 提前拉貨結
束，且 Monitor/NB 持續受記憶體與 IC缺貨影響，需求持續放緩，客
戶拉貨持續持保守態度，Mobility Solutions 及 Vertical Solutions 業務
表現相對持穩。整體 營收規模下滑， 三大業務獲利表現均較前季顯著
下滑，且公司持續在車用及新技術開發投入研究，使營業費用高於預
期，本業虧損擴大。  
4Q26 面板需求持續下滑 ，預期 Display 業務仍衰退：公司指引 4Q26
三大業務， Mobility Solutions 呈季持平，雖車市受零組件短缺及中國
內需市場保守並轉趨出口導向影響歐洲車廠，但公司車用業務仍相對
穩健； Vertical Solutions 若未考慮能源事業 併表影響下，季減低個位
數百分比；Display 業務延續 IT產品需求疲弱，客戶採購保守，短期
內記憶體及零組件缺貨影響無法緩解，預期仍呈現營收下滑 。受面板
業務持續下滑影響，我們預期 4Q26營收 638億元 (QoQ -4.59%)，毛利
率 10.46%(QoQ+0.06ppts) 。 
推動轉型，友達成立創新研究院，聚焦 前瞻技術：友達組織結構調整，
新設創新研究院，將著重在 MicroLED、光通訊、玻璃基板、 AR光波
導及低軌衛星通訊天線 。在短中長距離傳輸應用上，友達開發的光引
擎可依客戶需求提供 MicroLED 或 Micro VCSEL 技術方案，目前已與
客戶進行規格討論，並建置小量研發線，預計量產投資在一到兩年後
技術成熟時大規模展開。 而在玻璃基板方面，公司在鍍銅、 RDL佈線
及 TGV皆有技術佈局， 玻璃尺寸包含 510x515mm、310x310mm 等規
格，並持續開發相關技術。

### Page 1

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /10 /30

1

前日收盤價                                   683 .00元

洪士勛 
chris.hong@ctbcsis.com

封測  
日月光(3711 TT)           法說 /座談摘要  
3Q26財報表現亮眼，營收與毛利率 皆優於市場預期  
⚫ 打線與傳統先進封裝供給持續吃緊，定價能力強   
⚫ LEAP營收目標上修， 2027年達 10億美元以上  
⚫ CPO將於 4Q26量產， 2027年業務營收貢獻超過 5億美元。  
結論：3Q26財報表現亮眼，合併營收達 2,441.61億元，毛利率 22.1%，
稅後 EPS為 5.84元，皆優於市場預期。3Q26 ATM毛利率達 30.6%突破
過往上限，公司表示將以 30%作為未來的毛利率基礎。
LEAP(Leading -Edge Advanced Packaging) 與一般傳統封測業務均呈現
強勁成長，打線與傳統先進封裝產能供給持續吃緊，市場整體處於友善
之定價環境。展望 4Q26 營運，預估合併營收季增 6%~8%，毛利率
23.0%~24.0%，營業利益率 14.5%~15.5%。因持續積極佈局 LEAP與主
流先進封測，將 2026年全年資本支出由 105億美元追加至 115億美元，
公司也正向看待未來除了 AI 資料中心外長遠的前瞻應用需求。整體而
言，我們持續看好公司未來在先進封裝業務之成長表現。

一、3Q26財報概況：  
1.合併營收 2,441.61 億元 (QoQ+27.8% ， YoY+44.8%)，毛利率
22.1%(QoQ+1.1ppts，YoY+5.0ppts)。  
2.營業利益 331.42 億元 (QoQ+56.9%， YoY+151.1%)；營業利益率
13.6%(QoQ+2.5ppts，YoY+5.8ppts)。  
3.稅後淨利 269.96億元(QoQ+28.2%，YoY+148.4%)，稅後淨利率 11.1%。  
4.稀釋後每股盈餘 5.84元。

二、3Q26營運概況：   
1.ATM(半導體封測 )營收 1,514.85 億元(QoQ+20%，YoY+51%)，毛利率
30.6%(QoQ+3.3ppts)，突破過去的上限，公司也宣布以 30%作為未來毛
利率基底，營業淨利率 20.0%。依封測業務產品別比重為，
Bump/FC/WLP/SiP 占 51%(QoQ+2ppts)、打線封裝占 24%、測試占
17%(QoQ -2ppts)、材料占 2%、其它占 6%。

2.EMS(電子代工服務 )營收 934.69億元(QoQ+42%，YoY+35%)，EMS毛
利率 8.3%(QoQ -0.6ppts)，營業淨利率 3.3%(QoQ+0.9ppts)，因高單價零
組件 (如記憶體 )上漲墊高營收分母影響毛利率，但受惠於消費性電子季
節性旺季與 AI加速器電腦業務帶動營收規模擴大，營業淨利率提升。

3.營收比重 (依產品類別 ):  
ATM:通訊占 38%(QoQ -3ppts)、電腦占 34%(QoQ+4ppts，受 AI與 LEAP
帶動)、汽車 /消費性電子及其它占 28%(QoQ -1ppts)。 
EMS:消費性電子占 41%(QoQ+5ppts)、電腦占 21%(QoQ+5ppts，受 AI
加速器業務帶動 )、通訊占 19%(QoQ -3ppts)、工業用占 12%(QoQ -4ppts)、
汽車電子占 6%(QoQ -2ppts)、其它占 1%(QoQ -1ppts)。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /10 /30

2

4.資本支出：前三季累計資本支出達 80億美元 (其中設備 48億美元，設施 32億美元 )；3Q26
單季機器設備支出 21.04億美元、廠房設施 18億美元。 2026全年資本支出由 105億美元追
加至 115億美元 (其中設備與設施各加 5億美元，新增設備 60%用於 LEAP、40%用於主流封
測)。

三、營運展望：  
1.4Q26財務指引 (以 1美金兌換 31.9元新台幣假設 ): 
投控合併：以台幣計價，季營收 QoQ +6%~+8%，毛利率介於 23.0%~24.0%，營業利益率介
於 14.5%~15.5%。  
半導體封測事業：以台幣計價，季營收 QoQ+11%~+13%，毛利率 QoQ+1.0~+2.0ppts。 
電子代工服務：以台幣計價，季營收季對季持平，營業利益率約為 2.0%。 
2.CPO業務：公司表示 CPO部分將於 4Q26正式進入量產階段，預計 2027年 CPO相關業務
營收貢獻將超過 5億美元。公司認為 CPO與 NPO在未來將並存發展。 CPO將主要解決晶片
在極限頻寬、散熱與高功耗下的關鍵瓶頸。  
3.LEAP業務： 2026全年 LEAP營收目標由 37億美元上修至 40億美元以上，並維持 2027
年營收翻倍目標其中 Full Process 先進封裝預計 2026年貢獻 3億美元，2027年達 10億美元
以上。  
4.2027年資本支出：公司預期 2027年資本支出金額將比 2026年更高，並表示不吝於進行必  
要投資，以滿足客戶強勁需求並鞏固市場絕對領先地位。

四、公司 /產業看法：  
1.硬體基礎設施成新瓶頸：過去半導體瓶頸多在晶片設計或特定製程，但當前 AI對硬體尺
寸與複雜度的極限要求，導致目前硬體基礎設施成為全產業最嚴重的限制因素， 3Q26封測
產能接近滿載運作，目前短期成長的最大限制主要來自廠房空間與機器設備的安裝交付進
度。 
2.CPO與 NPO的未來：公司認為 CPO與 NPO未來將會並存，兩者主要差異在於整體封裝
效能表現，且公司與領先的晶圓代工夥伴及主要客戶保持深度合作，若未來 NPO出現明確
市場需求，同樣能發揮技術優勢成為主要參與者。  
3.下一代終端設備的微型化與系統化轉型：長遠來看，電子產品正在經歷在晶片層級實現系
統化與微型化的演進。未來的終端應用 (包含 Agentic AI、實體 AI/人形機器人等 )將整合更多
感測器、類比與混合訊號晶片，這些技術轉型將為傳統與先進封裝帶來長期穩健的結構性需
求。 
4.車用與工業用市場復甦 :儘管智慧型手機與 PC等傳統終端消費產品受到零組件價格偏高影
響而處於較平緩的階段，但車用與工業用市場的需求已展現復甦與成長動能。
